This is a beautiful recurrence. q sequence is the square root (formal power series) of the all-ones sequence, truncated: Q(z)^2 ≡ 1 + z + z^2 + ... + z^{m-1} mod z^m. The coefficients q_j are the binomial central? Let's compute: Q(z)=sqrt((1-z^m)/(1-z)) mod z^m. For infinite series, Q(z)=sqrt(1/(1-z)) = (1-z)^{-1/2}; coefficients q_j = binom(2j,j)/4^j. Indeed coefficients of (1-z)^-1/2: 1,1/2,3/8,5/16,35/128,63/256,... Our q for m=5 from right: q0=1, q1=1/2, q2=3/8, q3=5/16, q4=35/128. Yes! - Compute q = correlate via FFT: p_rev = p[::-1], conv = fft_convolve(p_rev, w) (length 3n-2). q = conv[n-1 : 2n-1]? Need ensure length n. - Update options:

But maybe we can solve its *dual* using a simple iterative algorithm because f is nonnegative and constraints linear. Could be equivalent to finding a nonnegative vector y minimizing ∑y_k subject to (F^T y)_i ≥1. Since y is over lags. If we choose y as a kernel, then g active where dual slack? But we can approximate the LP direction by taking g_i = f_i / (F^T y)_i for some y? From dual, if y is optimal, primal g has positive only where F^T y=1 (complementary slackness). Then g_i roughly inversely proportional. If we iterate both y and f, maybe.

Alternative: Use **multiplicative updates for polynomial square root**. There is an algorithm from "Newton's method for making coefficients flat": Given A, define error E_k = B_k - C. Update A_i ← A_i / (1 + λ ∑_{active k} sign(E) A_{k-i}). This is gradient. It may converge to optimal of some class. We can implement and hope.

Let's design final program around multiple strategies:

Let's design optimization routine in more detail.

Function `optimize_mult(n, p, rng, iters, t_start_factor, t_end_factor, rho, seed?)`:
- p float array length n, nonnegative, sum1.
- best_p, best_score (fast) current.
- for it in range(iters):
  - compute c=fast_convolve(p,p). cmax=c.max(); score_fast=2*n*cmax.
  - if score_fast<best_score: update best. Maybe call evaluate_sequence on converted list if improvement and time (exact) but expensive; can do final only.
  - if time step maybe check.
  - t = current t. To compute weights stable: exp((c-cmax)/t). If t very small, weights = zeros; set weights[argmax]=1. For stable, if cmax - c.min() > 100*t, use only near max.
  - q = correlation as above via FFT. This doubles FFT cost. We can combine? We already computed c=conv(p,p). Could compute q without FFT via direct for small active? But use FFT. For n=4096, each iteration 2 FFT convs of similar sizes; maybe 0.05s. 10000 iterations ~500s, okay.
  - grad = q/t.
  - eta = rho / max(1e-30, grad.max()). But if grad.max() changes. Use eta capped [0.01,2]. Maybe use `eta = min(1.0, rho / gmax)`.
  - new = p / (1.0 + eta * grad)
  - normalize. If any nan, break/revert.
  - Optional noise: if it % 50 ==0: new *= exp(rng.normal(0, noise_scale, n)); normalize, clip.
  - p=new.
- After loop, return best_p.

But this always reduces high-gradient components; may overly low. We can use **momentum** in log-space: z = log(p+eps); z -= eta*grad; p=softmax(z- max to ensure positive). Softmax may blow if z large. Use p directly multiplicative is stable.

```
def fast_objective(p):
 s=p.sum()
 if s<=0: return inf
 c=fft_real_convolve(p,p)
 cmax=np.max(c)
 return 2*len(p)*cmax/(s*s)
```

   best=..., best_score=fast_objective(p)
   t = t_factor * best_score/(2n)? Since cmax=best/(2n), t=20*cmax initially.
   for it in range(max_iter):
      c=conv
      cmax=...
      sc=2n*cmax
      if sc<best: best...
      # t: maybe m=cmax*min(t_factor, max(final, factor*0.999^it))
      factor = np.exp(-it*0.002)
      t = cmax * max(1.0, t_factor*factor)
      w=softmax weights
      q=corr
      g=q/t
      gmax = g.max()
      eta = 0.2 / max(gmax,1e-12); eta=min(eta,1.0)
      new=p/(1+eta*g); sum; normalize
      # occasional noise
      p=new
   return best
```

    p=np.asarray(p,float); p[np.isnan]=0? clip.
    s=p.sum()
    if not finite or s<1e-15: return inf
    p=p/s? For score need not normalize? If p sum1. But if candidate not normalized, normalize internally? Fast score: c=conv(p,p); return 2*len*pmax/(s*s). This is scale invariant. But for optimization we require sum1. Use separate.

def normalize(p): ...

def make_central(n):
   q=np.empty(n); q[0]=1.0
   for j in range(1,n): q[j]=q[j-1]*(2*j-1)/(2*j)
   a=q[::-1]
   return a/a.sum()

def exact_score_list(lst):
   val=evaluate_sequence(lst)
   return val if finite else inf

def candidate_list_from_p(p, scale=10.0): return [float(x) for x in p*scale]

def optimize_array(p, deadline, rng, label):
   n=len(p); p=normalize(p)
   best=p.copy(); best_fast=fast_score_p(p) # p sum1 score=2n*cmax
   # use fast_score normalized:
   for it in range(1000000):
      if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
      c=real_convolve(p,p); c=np.maximum(c,0)
      cmax=float(c.max()); sc=2*n*cmax
      if sc<best_fast*(1-1e-12): best=p.copy(); best_fast=sc
      # every some time maybe exact? Not
      # t factor
      factor=20.0 * (0.9995 ** it)
      factor=max(2.0, min(factor,50.0))
      t=max(cmax*factor, 1e-300)
      # weights:
      z=(c-cmax)/t
      # avoid overflow
      z=np.clip(z,-50,50)
      w=np.exp(z); wsum=w.sum(); w/=wsum
      # if wsum zero? no
      pr=np.ascontiguousarray(p[::-1])
      conv=real_convolve(pr,w)
      q=conv[n-1:n-1+n]
      g=q/t
      gmax=np.max(g)
      if not finite: break
      eta=0.25/gmax if gmax>0 else 0.0
      eta=min(eta,1.0)
      # If eta too small, increase factor? 
      new=p/(1.0+eta*g)
      ns=new.sum()
      if not finite or ns<=0: new=best.copy(); p=best.copy(); continue
      p=new/ns
      # occasional noise
      if it % 50 ==0:
         noise=1.0 + 0.02*rng.normal(0,1,n)
         noise=np.maximum(noise,0.1)
         p*=noise; p/=p.sum()
      # if multiple restarts? maybe if oscillatory
   return best,best_fast
```

Detailed helper implementations:

```
def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```
For convolution length L=a.size+b.size-1. Nft = next_pow2(L).

`real_convolve(a,b)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float); b=np.asarray(b,float)
L=a.size+b.size-1
nft=next_pow2(L)
ra=np.fft.rfft(a,nft)
rb=np.fft.rfft(b,nft)
out=np.fft.irfft(ra*rb,nft)
return out[:L]
```
Could be reused; fast.

But computing `irfft` returns length nft; slice. For p*p, a=b, can use power of one rfft to save. Implement `real_autocorr_square(p)`:

nft=next_pow2(2*n-1)
fa=np.fft.rfft(p,nft)
out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,nft)[:2*n-1]
```
Use for speed.

Let's code `run_local_search` with parameters.

```
def run_local_search(p0, deadline, rng, max_noise=0.03):
    p = normalize_array(p0)
    if p is None: return None, inf
    n=p.size
    best=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score_normalized(p)
    nft=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    t=10.0 # factor
    bad=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        # compute c
        fp=np.fft.rfft(p,nft)
        c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fp,nft)[:2*n-1]
        c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
        cmax=float(c.max())
        if not np.isfinite(cmax): break
        score=2.0*n*cmax
        if score < best_score:
            best=p.copy(); best_score=score; bad=0
        else:
            bad += 1
        if bad > 5000:
            p=best.copy()
            if rng.random()<0.5:
                # perturb
                idx = rng.integers(0,n)
                width=max(1,n//32)
                end=min(n,idx+width)
                p[idx:end] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5,end-idx))
                p=np.maximum(p,0); p/=p.sum()
            bad=0
            t=10.0
        # update weights
        factor = max(0.5, 10.0 * np.exp(-0.001*it))
        tt = max(cmax*factor, 1e-300)
        z=(c-cmax)/tt
        z=np.clip(z,-50,0) # since c<=cmax, but roundoff
        w=np.exp(z)
        wsum=w.sum()
        if wsum<=0 or not finite: w = np.zeros; w[np.argmax(c)]=1
        else: w/=wsum
        pr=np.ascontiguousarray(p[::-1])
        fw=np.fft.rfft(w,nft)
        fpr=np.fft.rfft(pr,nft)
        conv=np.fft.irfft(fw*fpr,nft)
        qgrad=conv[n-1:n-1+n]
        grad=qgrad/tt
        gmax=float(np.max(grad))
        if not finite or gmax<=0: it++; continue
        eta=min(1.0, 0.7/gmax)
        new = p / (1.0 + eta*grad)
        ns=new.sum()
        if not finite or ns<=0:
            p=best.copy(); continue
        p=new/ns
        if it % 100 ==0:
            # mild noise
            noise = 1.0 + 0.01*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
            noise=np.maximum(noise,0.1)
            p *= noise
            p *= (1.0/p.sum())
        it +=1
    return best,best_score
```
Need if `np.exp(-0.001*it)` for it maybe >100000 underflows? It becomes 0, max handles. Good.

def add_candidate(candidates, counter, p, score, cap=12):
    if p is None or not np.isfinite(score) or score>=np.inf: return
    p=normalize_array(p)
    if p is None: return
    candidates.append((float(score), counter, p))
    candidates.sort()
    if len(candidates)>cap:
        # keep best cap
        candidates[:] = candidates[:cap]
```

   if len(p) < n?

```python
"""
Search for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2

The code uses:
  * the sharp one-sided extremal family A(z) ~ (1-z)^(-1/2),
  * several perturbed starts around that family,
  * a soft-L_infinity multiplicative gradient search computed with FFTs,
  * a small population to avoid fixation,
  * final verification with the authoritative evaluate_sequence().
"""
import numpy as np
import time


def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_array(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return None
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    p /= s
    return p


def fast_score(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    n = p.size
    nft = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, nft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, nft)[:2 * n - 1]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s * s))


def make_central_unscaled(n):
    """Causal positive truncation of (1-z)^(-1/2), reversed so the end is largest."""
    q = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    q[0] = 1.0
    for j in range(1, n):
        q[j] = q[j - 1] * float(2 * j - 1) / float(2 * j)
    return q[::-1]


def make_central(n):
    q = make_central_unscaled(n)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return q / s


def add_candidate(candidates, counter, p, score, cap=16):
    if p is None:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    pn = normalize_array(p)
    if pn is None:
        return
    candidates.append((float(score), int(counter), pn))
    candidates.sort()
    if len(candidates) > cap:
        candidates[:] = candidates[:cap]


def random_start(rng, n, mode):
    if mode == 0:
        p = rng.exponential(1.0, n)
        return normalize_array(p)
    if mode == 1:
        p = 1.0 + rng.random(n)
        return normalize_array(p)
    if mode == 2:
        base = make_central_unscaled(n)
        noise = 1.0 + 0.15 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
        p = base * np.maximum(noise, 0.05)
        return normalize_array(p)
    if mode == 3:
        base = make_central_unscaled(n)
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        f = float(rng.integers(1, 25))
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        p = base * (1.0 + amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f * x + phase))
        return normalize_array(p)
    if mode == 4:
        m = int(rng.integers(3, 19))
        h = np.sort(rng.random(m)) ** float(rng.integers(1, 5))
        arr = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(m):
            a = i * n // m
            b = (i + 1) * n // m if i < m - 1 else n
            arr[a:b] = h[i]
        return normalize_array(arr)
    p = np.maximum(rng.normal(1.0, 0.6, n), 0.0)
    return normalize_array(p)


def run_local_search(p0, deadline, rng):
    p = normalize_array(p0)
    if p is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    n = p.size
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(np.fft.irfft(
        np.fft.rfft(p, next_pow2(2 * n - 1)) ** 2,
        next_pow2(2 * n - 1))[:2 * n - 1])))
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best_score = float("inf")

    nft = next_pow3 = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    t_factor = 9.0
    bad = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        fp = np.fft.rfft(p, nft)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, nft)[:2 * n - 1]
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        cmax = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(cmax):
            p = best.copy()
            bad += 1000
            continue

        score = float(2.0 * n * cmax)
        if score < best_score:
            best = p.copy()
            best_score = score
            bad = 0
        else:
            bad += 1

        if bad > 1500:
            p = best.copy()
            if rng.random() < 0.65:
                width = max(1, n // 64)
                start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
                end = min(n, start + width)
                p[start:end] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.6, end - start)))
                p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
                s = float(np.sum(p))
                if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s):
                    p /= s
                else:
                    p = best.copy()
            bad = 0
            t_factor = 12.0

        t_factor = max(0.1, t_factor * 0.995)
        tt = max(cmax * t_factor, 1e-300)
        z = (c - cmax) / tt
        z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
        w = np.exp(z)
        wsum = float(np.sum(w))
        if not np.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <= 0.0:
            w = np.zeros_like(c)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            w /= wsum

        pr = np.ascontiguousarray(p[::-1])
        fw = np.fft.rfft(w, nft)
        fpr = np.fft.rfft(pr, nft)
        conv = np.fft.irfft(fw * fpr, nft)
        qgrad = conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]
        grad = qgrad / tt
        gmax = float(np.max(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 0.0:
            it += 1
            continue

        eta = min(1.0, 0.7 / gmax)
        newp = p / (1.0 + eta * grad)
        ns = float(np.sum(newp))
        if not np.isfinite(ns) or ns <= 0.0:
            p = best.copy()
        else:
            p = newp / ns

        if it % 120 == 0:
            noise = 1.0 + 0.012 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
            noise = np.maximum(noise, 0.1)
            p *= noise
            s = float(np.sum(p))
            if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s):
                p /= s
            else:
                p = best.copy()

        it += 1

    return best, best_score


def scale_to_list(p, scale=10.0):
    p = normalize_array(p)
    if p is None:
        return []
    arr = np.clip(p * float(scale), 0.0, 1000.0)
    return [float(x) for x in arr]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    budget_s = max(0.0, float(budget_s))

    if budget_s >= 900:
        opt_n = 8192
    elif budget_s >= 300:
        opt_n = 4096
    elif budget_s >= 90:
        opt_n = 2048
    else:
        opt_n = 1024

    reserve = min(90.0, max(5.0, 0.05 * budget_s))
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, budget_s - reserve)

    # A strong deterministic fallback: the positive square root of 1/(1-z), truncated.
    q = make_central(opt_n)
    q_list = scale_to_list(q, 10.0)
    q_fast = fast_score(q)

    try:
        q_exact = float(evaluate_sequence(q_list))
    except Exception:
        q_exact = q_fast

    if not np.isfinite(q_exact):
        q_exact = q_fast
    best_exact = q_exact
    best_seq = q_list

    candidates = []
    counter = 0
    add_candidate(candidates, counter, q, q_fast, cap=18)
    counter += 1

    print(f"initial central score fast={q_fast:.9f} exact={best_exact:.9f} n={opt_n}", flush=True)

    # Add useful deterministic variations.
    qbase = make_central_unscaled(opt_n)
    x = np.arange(opt_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(opt_n)

    for g in (0.35, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8, 1.0, 1.2, 1.5, 2.0):
        p = np.power(qbase, float(g))
        sc = fast_score(p)
        add_candidate(candidates, counter, p, sc, cap=18)
        counter += 1

    for factor in (0.35, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8, 1.0, 1.15):
        p = qbase.copy()
        p[-1] *= factor
        sc = fast_score(p)
        add_candidate(candidates, counter, p, sc, cap=18)
        counter += 1

    for f0 in (1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13, 21, 34):
        for amp in (0.08, 0.16, 0.28, 0.42):
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            mod = 1.0 + amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f0 * x + phase)
            p = qbase * np.maximum(mod, 0.04)
            sc = fast_score(p)
            add_candidate(candidates, counter, p, sc, cap=18)
            counter += 1

    # A few smooth random starts.
    for _ in range(12):
        mode = int(rng.integers(0, 5))
        p = random_start(rng, opt_n, mode)
        if p is not None:
            sc = fast_score(p)
            add_candidate(candidates, counter, p, sc, cap=18)
            counter += 1

    # If a previously provided construction is available, include it if it is promising.
    try:
        hs = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
        hs = None

    if hs is not None:
        try:
            arr = normalize_array(hs)
            if arr is not None and arr.size >= 16:
                sc = fast_score(arr)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc <= max(q_fast, 1e-12) * 1.25:
                    add_candidate(candidates, counter, arr, sc, cap=18)
                    counter += 1
        except Exception:
            pass

    if len(candidates) == 0:
        add_candidate(candidates, counter, q, q_fast, cap=18)
        counter += 1

    slice_s = 45.0
    slices = 0
    last_best = candidates[0][0] if candidates else q_fast

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        candidates.sort()
        if len(candidates) == 0:
            break

        if len(candidates) == 1:
            idx = 0
        else:
            # Mostly follow the current leader, but keep exploring the neighborhood.
            if rng.random() < 0.78:
                idx = 0
            else:
                idx = int(rng.integers(0, min(len(candidates), 6)))

        p = candidates[idx][2].copy()
        end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + slice_s)
        if end <= time.monotonic():
            break

        bp, bs = run_local_search(p, end, rng)
        if bp is not None and np.isfinite(bs):
            add_candidate(candidates, counter, bp, bs, cap=18)
            counter += 1

        slices += 1

        if slices % 4 == 0:
            # Inject a fresh diversity seed occasionally.
            p = random_start(rng, opt_n, int(rng.integers(0, 5)))
            if p is not None:
                sc = fast_score(p)
                add_candidate(candidates, counter, p, sc, cap=18)
                counter += 1

        if candidates:
            cur_best = candidates[0][0]
            if slices % 8 == 0:
                print(
                    f"slices={slices} best_fast={cur_best:.9f} "
                    f"pool_size={len(candidates)} remaining={max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic()):.1f}s",
                    flush=True
                )
            if cur_best < last_best * (1.0 - 1e-6):
                last_best = cur_best

    # Final exact verification of the best few candidates.
    candidates.sort()
    checked = 0
    for score, _cnt, p in candidates:
        checked += 1
        if checked > 7:
            break
        if time.monotonic() > start_time + budget_s - 3.0:
            break
        lst = scale_to_list(p, 10.0)
        if not lst:
            continue
        try:
            val = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
        except Exception:
            val = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_exact:
            best_exact = val
            best_seq = lst

    # The authoritative fallback is still available.
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(q_list))
        if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_exact:
            best_exact = val
            best_seq = q_list
    except Exception:
        pass

    if not best_seq:
        best_seq = q_list
        best_exact = q_exact

    print(f"final exact score={best_exact:.9f} length={len(best_seq)}", flush=True)
    return best_seq
```
